台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓(02)5589-9558  (113) 金管投顧新字第 021 號 
公司拜訪快報

南亞(1303)：電子材料獲利與業外轉投資貢獻優於預期 
建議：維持買進

一、3Q26 自結獲利優於預期 
3Q26 營收季增 14.3%至 955.97 億元， 加上電子材料獲
利大幅成長，以及業外南亞科及台塑化挹注，單季自結
EPS 達 4.90 元、優於預期；累計前三季 EPS 為 10.07
元。各部門營益率中，電子材料自 2Q26 的 19.9%跳升
至 30.9%，塑膠加工則為 5.4%、化工部門 0.6%、聚酯
部門 0.2%。 化工與聚酯部門獲利衰退主要是2Q26 有低
價庫存，塑膠加工則因通膨干擾終端消費。然而，EG
在中國華東港口庫存嚴重偏低下，近一季報價及利差表
現好轉，抵銷可塑劑產品與 BPA 的虧損，聚酯部門則有
美國外銷動能挹注，支撐兩個部門 3Q26 小幅獲利。展
望 4Q26， 終端消費依舊沒有明顯的動能 ， 但EG 報價及
利差持穩且聚酯朝電子應用發展，管理層預期化工、聚
酯部門營益率與 3Q26 相當，電子材料在漲價延續及產
品組合優化下，營利率有望持續走升。

二、2027 年營運動能依舊為電子材料與南亞科貢獻 
3Q26 電子材料營收達 555 億元，其中，CCL 營收比重
達 31%、PCB(南電) 29%， 銅箔與Epoxy 則各佔 11%、
玻纖 7%。 獲利結構來看 ， 玻纖營益率走升至40%以上，
CCL 則季增 10 個百分點至 30%並高於 PCB 的 26%，
銅箔受惠加工費調漲達 15%，Epoxy 則維持 10~11%左
右。2027 年電子材料依舊是成長主力，部門營收比重將
達 60%以上，營益率有望在單季接近 40%的水準，但材
料漲價趨勢會從玻纖轉為 CCL。轉投資方面，南亞科因
記憶體市場供需缺口急遽放大 ， 預估將貢獻2026、2027
年稅前 EPS 9.17 與 16.74 元。

三、高階 CCL 訂單自 2H26 逐步出貨，2027 年效益將
明顯放大 
2025 年南亞 CCL 單價約每張 500 元，3Q26 已走升至
1,000~1,100 元/張，主要受惠漲價與產品組合優化。管
理層預期 4Q26 維持每月漲福 5~10%，且有機會延續至
1Q27。3Q26 中下旬 M 系列在 CCL 的營收比重由 20%
提升至 30%，漲價及產品組合優化都有影響，1Q27 在
M6、M7 訂單放量下會有更明顯的增長。目前 M4 CCL
單價約 1,100~1,200 元/張，M6 約當每張 2,200~2,300
元，M7 單價則跳升至 4,000 元以上 ， 有助2027 年 ASP
漲幅進一步擴大。此外，3Q26 的 CCL 產能利用率達
90~95%，現階段 CCL 月產能 618 萬張，規劃樹林、新
港將進行產線整改，預計 2026 年底跟明年可以各增加
約 15 萬張/月。 
四、維持買進理由 
3Q26 自結獲利優於預期，電子材料漲價與 CCL 產品組
合優化支撐本業中長期動能，轉投資南亞科(2408)與台
塑化(6505)大幅挹注業外收益 ， 上修2026、2027 年 EPS
分別為 15.72 及 29.24 元。考量獲利顯著成長有助股息
提高，且市場因公司獲利結構轉型而 Re-rating，維持買
進評等，目標價調升至 450 元(20x 2027 本業 EPS+10x 
2027 南亞科 EPS)。

### Page 3

Cathay’s View市場策略思維
市場焦點
1. 市場焦點:
• 中國記憶體技術突破、通膨升溫、AI融資擔憂, 形成亞股大跌風暴: AI融資及過度擴產的擔憂甚囂塵上,
加上殖利率攀升, 提高市場評價重調並放大檢視AI利空, 形成亞股重挫主因。
• 9月通膨超預期升溫, 市場擔憂10月再升1碼: CPI月增率創5個月來新高, 美債殖利率走高, 儘管聯準會
官員暗示10月無升息意願, 但市場超前反應升息可能, 使殖利率higher-for-longer。
• Q3財報季開跑, 預估獲利年增二成以上: 金融股率先公布Q3財報, 殖利率創高削弱固定收益交易收入,
但貸款需求穩定、景氣熱絡, 6大銀行營收成長仍創近年新高, 預估Q3標普500企業獲利年增逾二成。
• 台積電財報優於預期, 強調無過度投資疑慮: Q3營收獲利優於預期, 毛利率符合預期, 但上調全年預估
營收, 並強調CSP與資料中心無過度投資或擴產疑慮, 2026-2028年資本支出將遠高於過去三年。
2. 後續看點:
• 四大CSP財報(10/27-10/28)、FOMC利率會議(10/29)、ECB政策會議(10/29)、台灣GDP(10/30)

策略思維
1. 股市−多頭不變, 但高利率增添評價波動: 殖利率震盪上揚壓力增加, 市場持續調整股市評價面, 使股價落後
基本面表現。不過, AI趨勢明確, 企業營收獲利未來成長空間仍大, 應逢評價仍低時擴大布局。
2. 債市−長端不確定性仍高, 宜以中短天期或公司債為佳: 通膨高於預期, 長率仍有上行壓力。建議控制債券部
位存續期間, 選擇短天期或浮動債較佳。
3. 匯市−美元走揚與股市震盪, 引發台幣重貶: 美元雙率走高提高外資賣超台股疑慮, 台幣可能須承受額外壓力。

Cathay’s View
債務疑慮, 驅動CoreWeave債券利率飆升 大型CSP債券利率仍偏穩定
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/14。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/14。
• 美國9月通膨數據高於預期, 推升美債殖利率一度急彈, 進而引發市場對AI融資風險的疑慮。與此同時, 馬斯克及部
分創投人士也注意到CoreWeave債券殖利率飆升的問題, 並示警相關風險升高, 導致CoreWeave股價重挫15%。
• 不過, 對谷歌、亞馬遜、微軟等大型CSP業者而言, 儘管仍面臨現金流轉負的壓力, 但其發行的公司債價格仍相對
穩定, 並未因為CoreWeave事件而同步走高 , 反而逆勢走低 。這顯示, 目前AI融資疑慮仍主要集中在規模較小的
CSP業者, 尚未全面擴散至大型業者。然而, 隨著美債利率維持高檔, AI資本支出的融資壓力仍可能持續擾動市場。
科技｜CoreWeave債券利率飆升, 再度觸發AI融資敏感神經

### Page 6

6
Cathay’s View
• 隨美國10年公債殖利率站穩5%, 市場對AI過度投資的擔憂再起, 掀起AI拋售潮。但從產業基本面觀察, 四大CSP資
本支出仍在成長, 僅是年增率放緩, 台積電法說會亦表示沒有見到任何AI需求放緩的跡象, 意味即使AI成長放緩, 也
不代表缺少成長動能, 而是從初期萌發階段, 進入穩定成長期。
• 此外, 即便企業獲利增速放緩, 但只要能保持正成長, 普遍都可帶動指數上漲。以美台股市為例, 2021Q2為企業獲
利成長高峰, 但至2022Q3後股市才因升息與獲利轉負而下跌。近期股市大跌主要受市場重新調整估值與AI融資疑
慮影響, AI產業前景與企業基本面無虞, 逢拉回應站買方思考。

Cathay’s View
銀行股Q3財報狀況 摩根大通利息收入穩定, 但非利息收入受到債市拖累
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026Q3財報。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026Q3財報。
• 美國6大銀行Q3獲利仍具韌性, 其中放貸需求穩定成長, 但固定收益交易動能受到拖累。以商業銀行摩根大通為例,
放款總額成長10%至1.5兆美元, 存款亦有9%的成長, 使得利息收入270億美元, 年增12%, 為近10季最強動能。不
過, 由於美債利率高漲 , 固定收益交易收入年減 4%, 使得非利息收入 Q3僅成長3%。Q3整體收入為 502億美元,
EPS為5.57美元, 不及上季, 但仍創下同期新高。
• 就投資銀行高盛而言, 股票交易收入年增72%, 但固定收益的收入也年減1%, 使得整體收入為183億美元, 雖然仍有
28%的成長, 但動能也不及上季35%。摩根史坦利亦然, 手續費收入從上季的28%降至6%。
美股｜經濟保持韌性使利息收入穩定成長, 美債利率高漲影響固定收益收入, 使銀行財報表現分化

Cathay’s View
9月油價反彈, 同步推升CPI走揚 除能源反彈強勁, 商品與服務同步上升
註: 西德州原油(WTI)採每月均值之年增率計算。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9、2026/10。
註: 超級核心通膨為服務扣除租金。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9。
• 9月CPI年增率3.7%、核心CPI年增率2.6%雙雙高於預期(3.6%/2.5%)。受美伊衝突影響, 西德州原油(WTI)月均價
由8月82.5美元/桶升至9月95.7美元/桶, 推升汽油價格月增9.1%。此外, 核心商品、核心服務月增率均較前月升高;
細項方面, 租金(+0.4%)、醫療保健(+0.3%)、運輸(+0.6%)、二手車(+0.7%)升幅較大, 通膨壓力已非侷限於能源。
• 物價壓力由「單一油價衝擊」進一步擴散至商品與服務端 , 致使整體通膨降溫進程停滯 。在通膨黏性增強 、第二
輪傳導效應持續累積下, 若美伊情勢持續緊張, 高通膨維持時間將被迫拉長, 亦使美債利率持續面臨上行壓力。

Cathay’s View
CPI公布後, 升息預期先升後降 期限溢價同步推升10年美債利率
註: 聯邦利率期貨預估至2027年底約升息3碼。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/15。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/14。
• 9月CPI超預期, 利率先升後降: 10月升息機率一度上升至50%, 2年、10年債利率一度突破4.9%、5.3%。CPI公布
後, Fed官員仍對通膨保持警戒 , 不過Bowman表示無須立即行動 、Daly認為無需倉促決定升息 、Goolsbee與
Barkin傾向觀察更多數據; 官員談話緩和連續升息疑慮, 牽動10月升息機率回落至27%, 美債殖利率自高點回落。
• 通膨壓力未解, 升息預期將給予短端利率支撐; 長端利率則進一步受日漸墊高的期限溢價影響, 推升利率中樞水平
上移。中長期而言, 除了升息路徑的不確定性, 在財政赤字惡化、AI融資推升發債需求的背景下, 高利率環境仍將
成為市場常態。

• 高利環境推升民生融資壓力, 信用卡、車貸及房貸融資成本增加, 恐使拖欠率延續持續走升。此外, 亦影響耐久財

消費動能: 1)30年期房貸固定利率突破7.4%, 導致購屋與換屋門檻提高; 根據房地產經紀公司Redfin, 美國9月成屋
待售簽約量月減4%, 創2023年以來最大單月降幅, 待售房屋供給則升至2020年以來高點。2)油價走高亦增添購車
負擔, 壓縮相關消費意願。
• 本輪通膨源自能源供給、供應鏈重組等因素, 因此升息雖有助抑制需求, 但也將加重消費成本。未來若高利環境持
續影響家計單位消費, 將使本已分化的需求結構進一步惡化 , 加劇美國K型消費與資產表現分化 。即便Fed維持緊
縮立場, 但除了在控制通膨風險以外, 亦需兼顧是否抑制經濟成長、需求降溫過快的風險。

賭盤顯示民主黨贏得兩院選舉可能較大 政策情境分析: 若民主黨兩院勝選
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/14。 資料來源: Morgan Stanley, 國泰世華銀行整理。
• 美國期中選舉將於11/3舉行, 改選參眾議院與州長。由於油價大漲、通膨仍高, 共和黨與川普的民眾支持度持續下
降, 賭盤預期民主黨全面橫掃兩院的可能性上升, 扭轉目前由共和黨掌握兩院的局面。
• 若民主黨贏得兩院, 將提高國會制衡力道, 美伊衝突在選後全面開戰的可能下降; 其次, 財政政策將從減稅轉向補貼,
惟財政赤字是否改善仍須觀察; 值得留意的是, 若民主黨取得兩院與多數州長勝選, 可能提高資料中心反對建設聲
浪, 影響投資人信心。
• 然而, 並非所有共和黨都鼓勵資料中心。握有全美25%算力的德州州長Abbott有望連任, 雖是共和黨, 但政策已轉
向提高資料中心建設限制, 顯示當地資源緊迫性提升。
期中選舉｜民主黨橫掃兩院可能上升, 可能降低美伊再次開戰、但提高資料中心反彈聲浪

航運運費 油價走勢
原油｜地緣紛擾消息不斷, 油價高位震盪
• 面對11月即將到來的期中選舉壓力, 美國總統川普在燃料價格飆漲的逼迫下, 打出「聯俄壓油」牌, 不僅放寬制裁
向俄羅斯採購柴油, 更單方面高調宣布俄烏「能源停火」。然而, 克里姆林宮隨即轟炸烏克蘭能源設施, 戳破政治
秀的期待, 解決不了能源供應的近火。此外, 沙烏地基礎設施傳遭胡塞武裝組織轟炸, 更令市場擔憂。
• 雖然在期中選舉前, 川普雖可能持續透過政治干預、大釋戰備儲油來壓制油價, 但目前市場對這些政治動作似乎逐
漸鈍化, 加上航運運費持續攀高, 更不利能源價格放緩。
• 後市, 即便沙國以船對船等方式轉圜, 但地緣熱度難降、運費高踞, 期中選舉前政治壓力與原油供應的風險仍存, 油
價應仍維持高檔震盪格局。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/13。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/13。

營收獲利創新高, 毛利率走高 高毛利的服務營收大增為獲利動能關鍵
註: 預估範圍為2026Q4-2027Q1。
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/10/14。
註: 預估範圍為2026Q4-2027Q1。
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/10/14。
• ASML第三季營收116.6億歐元, 年增55%, 高於財測高標, 淨利49.6億歐元, 年增70%, 雙雙創下歷史新高。值得注
意的是, 毛利率57.45%高於前季財測的56%, 主要是高利潤的基礎安裝管理(Installed Base Management)銷售優
於預期, 反映晶片與記憶體製造商對既有產線的擴產需求強勁, 明年毛利率有望透過漲價而提升。
• 公司管理層表示, 部分訂單能見度已達2029-2030年, 且原定的2028年擴產30%已無法滿足客戶需求, 將上調產能。
過去ASML因客戶集中度高, 缺少議價能力, 但隨先進製程與封裝晶片需求大增, 客戶數量與需求成長, 並且對既有

產線更新的需求提高, 將帶動ASML後續營收與獲利成長。

通膨壓力下, 家計支出呈現負成長 實質薪資與殖利率同步走高
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/8。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9/30。
• 日本8月不含食物、能源的CPI年增1.9%, 但已公布的9月東京核心CPI(不含食物、能源)年增率由8月2%跳升至3%,
暗示日本9月CPI也呈上升趨勢, 這代表日本政府雖對油價進行大規模補貼 , 但民眾所承受的通膨壓力仍舊在上升 ,
家計支出也呈現連9個月負成長。
• 另一方面, 春鬥連三年漲薪5%以上, 實質薪資年增率終於在今年轉為正值, 民眾消費能力改善。隱含企業獲利好轉,
若搭配上日本10年期公債殖利率緩步走揚, 代表經濟呈現緩步復甦態勢。惟資金環境轉趨緊縮, 加上勞動力成本上
升, 除了AI相關企業獲利仍維持強勁成長動能外, 其餘行業將面臨消費承壓及融資成本上升的雙重挑戰。

• 三星電子第三季營業利潤率55%高於預期, 營業利潤107.4兆韓元則略低於預期, 主要受到韓元大幅升值影響 。此
外, 近期長鑫存儲發布首款4F²記憶體, 市場對於中國廠商擴產擔憂加劇, 使近期韓股表現弱勢。
• 然而, 產品發布到量產、良率合格仍需要很長的時間, 目前長鑫存儲整體技術仍落後三大廠2~3個世代。且高盛預
估中國記憶體需求至2028年將以50%的CAGR成長, 即便長鑫存儲積極擴產仍無法滿足中國自身需求, 市場對於中

國低價搶市佔的擔憂過大。此外, 美光先前財報會議時亦表示2027與2028的供需會較2026更加緊張, 三大廠持續
提高HBM產能佔比的趨勢不變, 記憶體廠未來兩年的獲利仍有持續成長的空間, 即便成長率放緩, 獲利維持時間會
遠較先前週期更長。
韓股｜匯率干擾使三星初步財報略低於預期, 但明後兩年獲利成長動能仍將延續

近期大部分市場參與者皆站在賣方 韓國個股槓桿ETF出現明顯資金流出
韓股｜回購買盤即將告終, 外資散戶同站賣方, 籌碼因素使韓股近期走弱
• 近期韓國股市走勢疲弱, 回檔幅度遠高於費半與台股, 除消息面的雜音外, 籌碼面也是原因之一。近期三星電子與
SK hynix的股票回購計畫即將額滿, 過去兩個月其它市場參與者皆站在賣方, 市場擔憂回購計畫結束後買盤將暫時
枯竭, 且韓國企業第三季的財報多數因匯率因素多數面臨下修, 在其他利空的干擾下加劇跌勢。
• 此外, 韓國個股槓桿ETF近期亦出現大量資金淨流出, 散戶在個股與槓桿ETF同步拋售, 顯示市場情緒偏向悲觀, 使
韓股評價已回落至歷史低值。然而, 即便未來兩年的獲利成長率將逐漸回落, 但在AI資本支出未歇的前提下, 韓國

企業的能夠維持在相對高位更久的時間(遠長於先前的循環), 現在進場買的是獲利的持久性而非成長率。但在股價
大幅修正後, 目前位階已具備較佳風險報酬比。

9月出口年增率大增至60%, 反映AI伺服器出貨改善 AI伺服器組裝商營收動能展望正向
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9。 註: 4大ODM指廣達、鴻海、緯創、緯穎。預估期間為2026Q4。資料來源: Bloomberg預估、公司官網, 截至2026/9。
• 台灣9月出口872億美元, 年增率大增至60.9%, 創今年3月來新高, 資通訊產品出口年增從前月的42%暴增至103%,
反映AI伺服器下游出貨改善, 電子零組件年增45%, 增速與出口金額均維持高檔, 反映AI晶片需求仍強勁。預期在
去年高基期之下, 即使Q4每月出口月增持平, Q4出口年增率仍有望達40%, 全年出口突破9000億美元。
• 四大ODM商9月營收總和年增 55.7%, 整體Q3台幣營收年增 59.1%, 小幅高於彭博預估的 58.7%。展望Q4, 四大
ODM營收年增率預估44%, 雖較前季放緩, 但考量目前仍是Vera Rubin出貨初期、台灣外銷訂單增速動能不墜, 後

續產能加速後, 營收成長有望維持或創佳, 也意味台灣Q4乃至明年出口動能仍強勁。

西班牙選舉民調 MSCI Spain vs Europe 權重拆解
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/5。 資料來源: MS, 截至2026/9/30。
歐洲｜西班牙選情利多金融股, 罷工風險成低接機會
• 民調顯示右派聯盟(PP、Vox)有望取得執政優勢(11/29大選), 市場預期新政府將取消銀行暴利稅、放寬金融監管並
重啟產業整併, 有利提升銀行獲利與資本運用效率。考量西班牙金融股占MSCI Spain權重高達45.5%, 遠高於歐洲
整體的26.9%, 在政策預期帶動下, 短期西班牙金融股乃至歐洲金融類股投資氣氛偏正向。
• 不過, 11月11日全國大罷工仍是重要觀察風險。近期民生壓力及勞資對立情緒升溫, 若抗議行動擴大, 可能影響之
後新政府改革推動速度。短期市場可望持續反映選舉紅利, 倘若罷工事件對金融股評價與歐股情緒的造成干擾, 加
上歐元區未來升息有利NIM, 反而是合適的切入點。

### Page 24

Cathay’s View
• 此前, 法國2027年財政預算案引發國會對峙, 法國CDS仍位於10月初以來的80點高位, 反映出市場對主權評級調降
與財政不確定性的政治風險溢價。然而, 這場債市風暴並非毫無防線。極右翼領袖馬琳·勒龐近期提出在2030年前
將赤字降至3%以下的影子財政計劃, 顯示即使政權可能輪替, 這為市場注入了關鍵的穩定劑 。預計信評機構下週
動作有限, 歐元短期應能止貶。
• 對歐元更核心的支撐 , 來自總體經濟的利差結構 。當前歐元區與美國的貨幣政策路徑並未出現實質性分歧 。隨著
兩大央行同步進入利率平台期或政策調整期 , 歐美升息與降息預期依然保持高度一致 。在雙方利差難以進一步擴
大的基本面大環境下, 歐元兌美元不具備單邊長期下跌的條件。

- N2 3%；N3 30%；N5 33%；N7 11%；先進製程技術(N7 及以下)占晶圓營收 77%

DCE 1%(QoQ+5%)

- 2026 年 CAPEX 上調至 US$60-64bn(原為 US$52-56bn，接近上緣)；70~80% 於先進製程，10% 特殊
製程、10~20% 先進封裝、測試光罩 
- 資本支出通常對應未來數年更高的成長機會，目前看未來三年 CAPEX 會比過去三年再更為強勁 
- 已提前與設備供應商規畫產能，目前不預期設備供應會成為擴產計畫的重大瓶頸  
- 宣布將在亞利桑那追加 US$100bn 投資(總額提高至 US$265bn)，新增數座 N2 及更先進製程邏輯晶
圓廠、先進封裝廠

- 先前提出的 2024-29 年 AI 相關營收「中高 50% CAGR」展望，管理層表示需求已變得「更強、更

元富證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市敦化南路二段 97 號 19 樓 (02)2325-3299  (1 15)金管投顧新字第 016 號 
公司拜訪快報

南亞科(2408) 
建議：維持買進 
結論：2027 年產業供需吃緊不變，長期 AI 營運占比挑
戰五成

一、南亞科為利基型 DRAM 製造廠，2026 年資本支出
上修至 697 億元(YoY+420%) 
南亞科為利基型 DRAM 製造商 ， 最大股東為台塑集團之
南亞塑膠持有 29.28%股權，主要競爭對手包含
Samsung、SK Hynix、Micron 以及華邦電，目前月產能
為 6.5 萬片，20nm 和 1B 為主力生產製程。依產品終端
應用區分，消費性電子和 AI 分別占營收比重為 60%+和
20%+。公司維持 2026 年銷售量年增 15-20%目標，因
5A 新廠需待 2H27 量產且存貨處於低水位 ， 故銷售量年
增率不如去年亮眼。至於在資本支出方面，2026 年資本
支出由 520 億元上修至 697 億元，其中五成將用於生產
設備 。 此外 ， 新廠將於1Q27 開始裝機 ，2H27 開始量產，
2028 年達成產能 30K， 新廠全產能由45K 上修至 50K，
投入資金為 4800 億元。 
二、客戶新 LTA 生效致使 ASP 季增幅超過六成，3Q26 
25.4 元續創新高 
3Q26 營收 1336 億元(+62%QoQ)，受惠於 LTA 長約價
格調漲，ASP 季增超過六十位數百分比，銷售量和匯率
則分別為季減中個位數和季增低個位數 ， 受惠於ASP 大
幅上揚且折舊費用持穩 ， 毛利率85.7 優於前季之 79.5%，
營業利益 1068.9 億元(+76%QoQ)，稅後淨利 876.2 億
元(+75%QoQ)，EPS 25.4 元續創新高。 
三、DRAM 供不應求延續，但 ASP 增幅將收斂，估計
4Q26 EPS 28.13 元 
DRAM 產業供不應求持續 ， 原廠供給持續向AI 傾斜 ， 由
於合約價已大幅調漲，ASP 增幅將收斂，估計 4Q26 營
收 1474.1 億元(+10.3%QoQ)，毛利率改善至 86.6%，
營業利益 1196.5 億元(+12%QoQ)，稅後淨利 970.4 億
元(+11%QoQ)，EPS 28.13 元。  
四、原廠新產能規劃搭配客戶長約需求，且 HBM 投片
占比增加，2027 年產業供不應求已成定局  
在產業需求方面，全球九大 CSP 2027 年 Capex 1.38
兆美元(+50%YoY)，AI 運算將主導 DRAM 需求成長，
帶動 DRAM 需求從 HBM、LPDDR5 擴展至網路 、 儲存，
至於傳統應用則面臨供給不足以及價格調漲，導致產品
降規或生命周期延長，部分低階市場需求則需仰賴回收
DRAM。在產業供給方面，雖供應商整體擴充產能，但
產能規劃將搭配客戶長約需求，有助長期供需穩定，加
計 HBM 於產品投片占比由今年 22%增加至明年之 30%，
致使 DRAM 新增產能主要用於 HBM 生產。故以整體市
況而言，AI 強勁需求將推動記憶體供需結構改變，降低
景氣循環對於供應商影響，產業庫存仍偏低，2027 年產
業供不應求可望延續。 
在 DRAM 報價走勢上 ， 雖消費端壓力未減 ， 但AI Server
增量需求支撐 4Q26 記憶體合約價漲勢 ， 集邦預估4Q26
傳統型 DRAM 價格季增 10-15%低於前季之 13-18%，
就南亞科而言 ， 營收占比達50%+的 LTA 客戶已於 3Q26
大幅調漲價格，故預期 4Q26 價格漲勢將趨緩。 
五、新廠產出效益於 2H27 開始發酵，長期 AI 產能占比
上看五成 
南亞科今年資本支出從 520 億元上修至 697 億元，主因
為生產設備投資增加，5A 新廠維持 1Q27 裝機規劃，
2028 年和 2030 年投片目標分別為 30K 和 50K， 整體新
廠投資規模達 4800 億元 ， 由於5A 新廠量產預計於 2H27，
故 4Q26-2Q27 出貨量約當 3Q26，至於屏東 3D 晶圓封
裝測試廠預期在 2028 年底前導入矽穿孔(TSV)和晶圓堆
疊等先進技術。此外，儘管資本支出積極，目前AI 基礎
設施(包含 Server、網路、儲存以及系統管理)客戶營收
占比超過 20%， 但新產能擴建主要以AI 客戶需求為主，
故經營階層預期 2029-2030 年 AI 營收占比將上看
40-50%。 
六、維持買進理由 
1)預估 2026 年和 2027 年 EPS 分別為 75.63 元和
119.26 元。 
2)考量 2027 年產業供需吃緊不變，長期 AI 營運占比挑
戰五成，維持買進評等，TP 660 元 (2027 PBR x 2.8)。

Takeaways on NPO from 2026
OCP Global Summit
Morgan Stanley Asia Limited+
Andy Meng, CFA
Equity Analyst
Andy.Meng@morganstanley.com                                                +852 2239-7689
Betty Chen
Research Associate
Betty.H.Chen@morganstanley.com                                                +852 2239-7213
Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
On Day 2 of the 2026 OCP Global Summit, an IPEC optical interconnect workshop

focused on discussions around NPO. Industry participants such as Source Photonics,
Lightcounting, Alibaba, Meta, Oracle, Microsoft, Ciena, Ligent, Genuine Optics,
Lumentum, Lumilens, NVIDIA and Huawei presented their views on NPO.
The industry has formed the view connectivity, rather than compute alone, is
becoming the primary bottleneck. Near-packaged optics (NPO) move optical
conversion closer to compute, targeting better signal integrity, bandwidth, and

energy efficiency. OpenCPX explicitly targets common mechanical, electrical,
optical, thermal, and management interfaces across suppliers, providing the
ecosystem with a shared implementation framework rather than only a broad
architectural vision.
However, we also observed debate over whether pluggable NPO offers materially
better flexibility and serviceability than CPO. Key concern on CPO is that optical
failure could result in scrapping an entire ASIC assembly.

Overall, we observed growing discussion and interests in NPO, implying a greater
likelihood of accelerated commercialization over the next 12-18 months, in our view.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley
Research as only a single factor in making their investment

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
Connectivity has become the bottleneck, and NPO is a potential solution. We
observed growing discussion and interest in NPO at the conference.
October 14, 2026 08:47 AM GMT

### Page 1

ab 15 October 2026
Global Research
First Read
Genius Electronic Optical
Private placement with 3M expands its optical
connectivity opportunity
Genius approved a private placement with 3M
On 15th Oct, Genius's board approved a private placement to 3M UK Limited for
5.54mn shares at NT$869.6 per share, an 18.3% discount to the 14th Oct closing price
NT$1,065, representing NT$4.8bn investment and 4.68% stake in Genius. Proceeds
from the transaction will fund technology development, working capital, and bank loan

repayment. The two companies also announced a strategic partnership to develop
optical communication for AI data center and HPC. We believe this announcement is a
positive validation for Genius working with a tier one supplier in optical connectivity to
provide its precision components for advanced optics.
3M EBO technology could open up a US$100mn TAM for Genius
3M has been developing its EBO (expanded beam optical) technology since 2019 with
commercialization in late 2024 and deployment for Microsoft Azure data centers in July

2026. EBO is an upgrade to MPO connectors at the ends of fiber optics cables.  The
EBO is a lensed, non-contact ferrule that uses a prism lens to expand and collimate a
light beam to send across fiber before refocusing into a receiving fiber, improving dust
tolerance to cut cleaning steps and cable inspection times 85%. The technology was
supported by the MSA launched in May 2026, co-chaired by Oracle, with Meta, Senko
and Molex among the members for scale-out cabling. We saw opportunity opened for

Genius on the EBO ferrule, a small molded plastic part with a row of micro-lenses to
widen the light beam plus an anti reflection coating. This could open up a US$100mn
market TAM for Genius based on 4-12 ferrules per GPU (2-3 connections x 2-4 plugs per
connection) x 20mn accelerators x US$1 for the lens assembly.
Validation for its optical solution push with potential hyperscaler exposure
We believe the scale of placement at NT$4.8bn suggests scope to expand into CPO

where it could improve the MT ferrule used as the detachable plug end of the FAU to
hold the fiber in position when it is connected to a switch adapter or removable cable.
Genius  collaboration with 3M can bring the company closer to the end demand with its
direct partnership with Microsoft and bring revenue contribution forward to 2027,
ahead of Genius CPO ramp in 2028E. For CPO, we still see potential to reach 10% of the
TAM supplying MT ferrule, prism and V-groove.

Reiterate Buy with PT at NT$1,250
Our recent update on Genius had raised the PT from NT$700 to NT$1,250 reflecting the
company's potential CPO market with its progress sampling three precision components
inside the fiber array unit (FAU) targeting FAU integrators such as Coherent and FOCI.
The collaboration strengthens our confidence on Genius capability within the optical
connectivity industry, and reiterate our Buy on the compony.

Company Description
Founded in 1989, Genius Electronic Optical (GSEO) is a leading global manufacturer of
camera lenses, providing full service optical solutions, including mobile lenses, automotive
lenses and AR/VR optics. Its production sites are in Taichung (one plant) and Xiamen (two

plants). The Taichung plant mainly manufactures VR lenses, while the Xiamen plants produce
compact lenses for consumer electronics. Optical lens products accounted for 73% of 2023
total revenue, and others include TOF lenses, flash lenses, VR lenses and LED lighting
products. Its major client is Apple and it supplies lenses for iPhones and iPads.
UBS EPS and Consensus EPS
The UBS EPS is an adjusted diluted EPS metric. It is calculated using the UBS analyst's

interpretation of earnings suitable for valuation purposes divided by the diluted number of
shares. This may differ to the way the consensus EPS metric has been calculated.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our price target is based on PE-based sum-of-the-parts model.
Genius' growth is highly leveraged to the overall handset industry and could be affected by
volatility in handset demand, as well as whether handset OEMs will continue upgrading their

camera design. Genius' margins could be affected by increasing competition and the rise of
new technologies, ASP pressure from customers and fluctuating material and labour costs.
Downside risks include competitors catching up in technology, yield rate issues for new
products and uncertain iPhone demand.

Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

Background: Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT), the world’s largest PCB 
manufacturer by revenue, generated revenue of TWD182.5bn in 2025. 
Historically focused on consumer electronics and smartphones, the 
company is shifting towards higher-value AI-server PCBs (HLC/iHDI), 
optical-module PCBs (mSAP), and IC substrates (ABF/BT). These 
businesses accounted for 21.6% of its revenue in 1H26, up from 11.7% in 
2025. Management targets this ratio to reach c.25% in 2026 and c.30% in 
2027. To support this shift, ZDT has 22 fabs under construction and plans 
to build nine more, primarily for mSAP/HDI, HLC, and ABF substrates.

Highlights: ZDT’s “One ZDT strategy” is gradually bearing fruit. Since 
2018, management has pursued its “One ZDT strategy” to broaden the 
product portfolio – including HLC, substrates, mSAP/HDI, rigid-flex and 
SLP – by leveraging its strengths in FPCB and consumer HDI. This 
strategy is beginning to pay off, and management expects its fast-growing 
segments, including mSAP HDI for optical transceivers, AI-server HDI and 
ABF/BT substrates, to gain momentum from this year. Management 
expects revenue from these segments to increase by c.150% YoY and 
account for c.25% of total revenue (or c.TWD50bn) in 2026, and to further 
increase by 100% YoY to TWD100bn in 2027. ZDT targets these fast-
growing segments to reach 30% of its revenue mix in 2027 and c.45-50% 
in 2030. Given current progress, the 2030 target could be reached in 2028. 
For mSAP HDI, management expects the 800G/1.6T transceiver product 
mix to shift to 7:3 in 2027, from 5:5 in 2026, and aims to become the largest 
supplier of optical-transceiver HDI in 2027. In AI-server HDI, we expect the 
2 leading ASIC vendors to gradually shift from pure HLC to thick HDI 
boards – a combination of HLC and HDI – in their next-generation designs, 
with ZDT a potential beneficiary. This could drive c.3x growth in ZDT’s 
business in 2027.

Product mix to support gross margin expansion. Margin expansion is 
increasingly driven by a favourable product mix. Gross margin reached 
22.4% in 1H26 (vs. 16.5% in 1H25). Management indicated that optical-
transceiver HDIs currently have the highest gross margin, at over 40%, 
followed by IC substrates, with ABF margins potentially reaching 50-60% 
and BT margins around 30% as a long-term target. Server HLC margins 
are around 20-30%, while HDI margins should be higher given their greater 
process complexity. With margins in traditional businesses at c.20%, ZDT 
expects growth in the newer segments to drive further margin expansion.

Valuation: The stock is trading at 36/19x PER for 2026/27E based on 
Bloomberg consensus. It’s past 3-year PER range is 10-30x.

20 October 2026

Business transformation bears fruit

➢ Key beneficiary of growth in optical transceiver & AI server HDI demand  
➢ Aggressive substrate capacity strategy paying off in current conditions   
➢ Margin expansion on fast-growing segments (HDI and substrates)

水冷機櫃與機殼本業同步走升 
 2Q26 本業穩健成長 ，EPS 1.41 元：晟銘電 2Q26 營收 32.19 億元，
年增 22.5%，毛利率則從前期 21.1%降至 20.5%，主要是因客戶
Storage 相關產品出貨增加，然 Storage 產品已進入成熟階段，客戶
持續每季議價，2Q26 稅後淨利為 2.9 億元，EPS 1.41 元。 
 預期 3Q26 為營運谷底，4Q26 開始爬升：展望 26H2，3Q26 營收年
增 16%，估計 3Q26 稅後 EPS 1.49 元。4Q 通常為傳統旺季，加上
VR200 也會在 4Q26 陸續加入量產，而晟銘電也有參與其中，出貨
量將逐步上升 ， 隨著 VR200 及 ASIC 等新平台陸續進入放量階段，
預期機殼與 Sidecar 出貨量將同步提升。此外，2026 年 ODM 專案
有 8 至 9 成聚焦機櫃業務，涵蓋 OCP、EIA 以及 MGX，預期 L11
系統整合業務營收將進一步提升。 
 維持買進，目標價 140 元：投顧認為晟銘電 4Q26 起受惠 VR200 機
殼與 ASIC 放量，2027 年營運可望進一步成長，且公司已成功切入
Meta 供應鏈，並在冷卻機櫃與機構件取得關鍵認證，可直接承接
Meta 釋出的巨額硬體訂單，且在液冷架構成為主流帶動下，晟銘電
水冷相關業務比重持續提升 ， 使2026 年整體毛利率有望升到 20%以
上 ， 投顧預估2026 年 EPS 6.13 元，2027 年 EPS 9.4 元 ， 維持買進 ，
目標價 140 元。

個股報告

晟銘電 為伺服器機殼大廠，目前
切入 IT 機櫃、水冷機櫃、水冷搭
配的分歧管 
 晟銘電子科技股份有限公司成立於 1976 年 6 月 ， 為台灣伺服器機殼OEM/ODM
製造商， 公司主要銷售電腦機殼及伺服器機殼 ，其用途為桌上型電腦及伺服器等
各項組件之安裝防護設備 ， 近年來公司積極跨足散熱領域並研發設計出液冷散熱
的浸沒式散熱產品。於 2024 年公司推出多款液冷機櫃及液冷散熱系統。晟銘電
提供全球客戶迅速且彈性的 OEM 及 ODM 生產服務。2026 年 1~8 月主要產品
營收占比，伺服器 73%、L11 系統整合 18%、Tooling 6%、DT 3%。

Exhibit 3: 產品組合

Sources : Company、MasterLink

2Q26 本業穩健成長，EPS 1.41
元 
 晟銘電 2Q26 營收 32.19 億元，年增 22.5%，毛利率則從前期 21.1%降至
20.5%，主要是因客戶 Storage 相關產品出貨增加，然 Storage 產品已進入成熟
階段，客戶持續每季議價，2Q26 稅後淨利為 2.9 億元，EPS 1.41 元。

預期 3Q26 為營運谷底，4Q26
開始爬升 
 展望 26H2，3Q26 營收年增 16%，估計 3Q26 稅後 EPS 1.49 元。4Q 通常為
傳統旺季，加上 VR200 也會在 4Q26 陸續加入量產，而晟銘電也有參與其中，
出貨量將逐步上升，隨著 VR200 及 ASIC 等新平台陸續進入放量階段，預期
機殼與 Sidecar 出貨量將同步提升。此外，2026 年 ODM 專案有 8 至 9 成聚焦
機櫃業務，涵蓋 OCP、EIA 以及 MGX，預期 L11 系統整合業務營收將進一步
提升。

今年前八月 L11 系統組裝營收占比已達 18％ ， 而明年L11 系統組裝營收貢獻還
會持續提升。公司在機櫃產品線已相當完整，還可提供配套的水冷配件，機箱
基本上是跟隨著輝達的腳步走 ， 包括GB300、VR200 等標準品機箱都已經開好
模具。海外產能布局，公司目前生產基地仍以中國大陸為主，其中東莞為主要
生產基地，另於寧波設有產能，產品以機殼、機櫃為主，東莞廠自動烤漆線預
計今年底前完成，一天可以完成60 個機櫃烤漆；台灣中壢則主要負責打樣及水
冷產品研發，部分零組件仍由中國生產後運送至中壢進行後段組裝。泰國一期
廠已完成建置並具備生產條件 ， 二期則持續進行設備調校 ， 主要導入烤漆產線，
預計 4Q26 開始試產，後續待客戶完成稽核及認證後逐步量產。泰國二期的烤

漆設備規格除可支援機殼外，亦可延伸至機櫃產品，有助未來將部分機櫃製程
移往海外。今年中壢廠、泰國廠都有新產能加入，而明年資本支出大致也會維
持在大約 7～8 億元。

維持買進，目標價 140 元 
 投顧認為晟銘電 4Q26 起受惠 VR200 機殼與 ASIC 放量，2027 年營運可望進
一步成長 ， 且公司已成功切入Meta 供應鏈 ， 並在冷卻機櫃與機構件取得關鍵認
證，可直接承接Meta 釋出的巨額硬體訂單，且在液冷架構成為主流帶動下，晟
銘電水冷相關業務比重持續提升，使 2026 年整體毛利率有望升到 20%以上，
投顧預估 2026 年 EPS 6. 13 元，2027 年 EPS 9.4 元，維持買進，目標價 140
元。(15x2027EPS)

1) 客戶機箱導入狀況。

### Page 1

Company Report 
公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場
或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

1 
6274 台燿 
(Taiwan Union Technology Co.)

報告日期： 2026/10/08 研 究 員：方姿婷  (Lesley Fang) 
壹、投 資 建 議 
重點摘要： 
 2Q26 營收符合預期， 毛利率受 1H26 漲價提升 ， 預期 3Q26 毛利
將順利反應成本上漲與優化之產品組合 ， 毛利率預估提升 3%+ 。  
 AI 伺服器帶動高階銅箔基板需求強勁，同時驅動高階網路交換
器升級動能，銅箔基板需要更高速傳輸、訊號低損耗之下會陸
續採用 CCL M8、M9 規格。 
 2H26 低軌 S 客戶 V3 版本新型火箭搭載低軌衛星，所採用之銅
箔基板升級至 M8 等級；以及，1.6T 光模組需求量增帶動銅箔
基板需求量增。

一. 2Q26 營收與財報符合市場預期，3Q26 營收優於預期  
台燿 2Q26 營收為 143.01 億元，+42.2%QoQ、+110.93%YoY，毛利
率為 29.72% ，稅後淨利為 23.42 億元，單季稅後 EPS+7.82 元。2Q26
台燿受到 ASIC CCL 專案出貨帶動，產品組合優化，毛利率已相較
1Q26 季增 4.5% 。台燿已公告 3Q26 營收為 179.48 億元(優於市場預
期 3%)，+25.50%QoQ，毛利率可順利轉嫁且高階 CCL 出貨比重提
升，預估台燿 3Q26 毛利率為 33.21%，稅後淨利 36.72 億元，單季
稅後 EPS+12.60 元。

二. AI 伺服器帶動 CCL/PCB 產值提升，產能擴充 33% 
宏遠投顧觀察，AI 伺服器與先進封裝等需求日增，高階 CCL 市場
需求將在 2024 年~2026 年 CAGR 達 26% ， 而高階 CCL 供給端 CAGR
則是 7%，高階市場需求殷切。高階銅箔基板(包含 AI 伺服器和高
階網路交換器，自 2024 年始採用 M8 CCL 材料；2026 年 M8 材料
佔總體 CCL 市場 40%，M8 材料佔比顯著高於 2022 年、2023 年的
22%和 26%。台系銅箔基板供應鏈當中，M6~M8 等高階材料的供
應廠商為(2383)台光電為首、(6274)台燿次之。回顧 3 年來，台燿
產能擴充進程，端視泰國廠進度而定。4Q26 泰國新廠產能陸續開
出，台燿總產能為 260 萬張/月，+13%YoY。展望 2027 年，台燿泰
國廠、常熟廠初步先提升 30 萬張/月，逐步提升總新增產能達 120
萬張/月，預估 2027 年底時，總產能達 380 萬張/月，+46%YoY；預
估 2028 年底時，台燿總產能擴充至 530 萬張/月，+39.5%YoY，平
均 3 年每年產能擴充約 33% 。高階 CCL 在 AI 伺服器、高速網路交
投資建議：買進

換器(高階 Switch)帶動之下，一旦，泰國廠產能擴充可望使得台燿
市佔率得以提升 。 2027 年隨著 NVIDIA Rubin 平台將帶動 CCL M8+ 、
M9 材料出貨攀升，AI ASIC CCL 拉貨動能呈現 50~60%高度成長，
同業台光電與台燿高階 CCL 新產能釋出 ， 產品組合持續優化 。 2027
年起 ， 除一般型伺服器 Intel Diamond Rapids 平台 、 AMD Zen6 Venice
平台採用 PCIe Gen6 與 Ultra Low Loss CCL(M7)，PCB 板層數增至
20~24L；2027 年，隨著 1.6T Switch 逐步導入 M8/M9 CCL(HLC 層
數 46~52L、玻纖 Low Dk2/Q 布、銅箔 HVLP4/5) ，預期 CCL 廠商產
品規格顯著升級。

三.  台燿高階網路 Switch 板材、低軌衛星板材、1.6T 光
模塊板材，27 年規格朝向高階型號發展 
展望 2H26，在高階 Switch 升級方面，800G Switch 採用 M8、M9 
CCL 規格，2H26 台燿將新增高階 Switch 客戶，隨著 AI 伺服器、
高階網路交換器產業發展朝向 800G、1.6T Switch 升級趨勢帶動之
下， 
銅箔基板需要更高速傳輸、訊號低損耗之下會陸續採用 CCL M8、
M9 規格。此外，2H26 開始 800G 與 1.6T 光纖收發模組(採用 M8、
M9 CCL)開始放量，AI 資料中心帶動光纖收發模組需求，400G 以
上光纖收發模組出貨量 2026 年 1 億個、2027 年 1.45 億個、2028 年
達到 1.6~1.65 億個；在中系系統廠商積極拉貨之下，1.6T 光纖收
發模組 2026/2027/2028 年估量 15~20M/55~60M/85~100M ， 1.6T 光纖
收發模組，於 2 年內呈倍數增長。低軌大型 S 客戶 V3 版本新型火
箭搭載低軌衛星 ， 所採用之銅箔基板升級至 M8 等級 ， 材料損耗要
求降低，訊號傳出完整性標準提高，對於 CCL、PCB(HDI)、玻纖
與銅箔等規格預期同步升級，推升材料成本與 PCB 產值。

四.預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 38.49/66.22 元 
2026 年受惠 AI 伺服器及 ASIC 需求持續強勁的帶動下，台燿為網
通、高階網路交換器銅箔基板重要供貨廠商，將受惠 AI Server 對
於高階銅箔基板強勁的需求，因而，預估台燿 2026 年營收 618.78
億元，+108.01%YoY，毛利率 31.13%，稅後淨利 114.79 億元，稅後
EPS+38.49元 。 隨泰國廠產能釋出 ， 預估台燿 2027年營收增至 963.61
億元，+55.73%YoY，毛利率為 33.26%，稅後淨利 198.23 億元，稅
後 EPS+66.22 元。

四.投資建議維持買進，目標價 2,150 元 
宏遠投顧認為，台燿營運重點在於，營運動能於 2026 年起加速啟
動，一般型伺服器 CPU 廠商 Intel 、AMD 均有新品帶動新平台升級
與相關供應的零組件商機，以及攻入 AI 伺服器、AI 高階網路交換

一. 公司簡介

電路板之銅箔基板 、 內層銅箔基板 、 黏合片 ， 產品應用別當中 ， ELL
與 SLL(M7、M8)佔比 39%、VLL 與 LL(M4、M6)佔 31%、HDI 與 N-
LL 佔 16%、其他品項佔 14%。

二.2026~2027 年營運概況及展望 
1.HDI 與 M8 以上升級仍為關注焦點，2026~2028 年產能建置積極 
過往一般型伺服器用板採用 HDI 設計，強調細線路設計，CPU 尺
寸變大 Pin Count 數量增加至 2,000 只~4,000 只 Pin Count，PCB 板
面積不變之下要走向細線路化(線寬/線距)朝向更細線路設計。製
程上 HDI 經過多次壓合後，PP 在熱壓多次後，每一層熱膨脹係數
不同，導致通孔 Crack 或翹曲，製作時容易產生爆板，因此對於
PCB 板廠製作良率會是一大技術難關，而金像電、健鼎等廠商於
高階 HDI 相對具備生產優勢與競爭力，相對應台光電、台燿等高
階 CCL 供貨與升級也成為製程一大瓶頸；回顧 3 年來，台燿產能
擴充進程，端視泰國廠進度而定。4Q26 泰國新廠產能陸續開出，
台 燿 總 產 能 為 260 萬 張 /月，+13%YoY。展 望 2027 年，台 燿 泰 國 廠、
常熟廠初步先提升 30 萬張/月，逐步提升總新增產能達 120 萬張/
月 ， 預估 2027 年底時 ， 總產能達 380 萬張/月 ， +46%YoY ； 預估 2028
年底時，台燿總產能擴充至 530 萬張/月，+39.5%YoY，平均 3 年
每年產能擴充約 33%。高階 CCL 在 AI 伺服器、高速網路交換器
(高階 Switch)帶動之下，一旦，泰國廠產能擴充可望使得台燿市佔
率得以提升。2027 年隨著 Nvidia Rubin 平台將帶動 CCL M8+、M9
材料出貨攀升，AI ASIC CCL 拉貨動能呈現 50~60%高度成長，台
光電與台燿等高階 CCL 新產能釋出，產品組合持續優化。

針對 AI GPU 與 ASIC 發展進程，Google TPU 出貨量從 2026 年 400
萬顆上修至 2027 年(V8 增量)增至 800 萬顆，倍數增長。AWS ASIC 
預計 2027 年出貨量達 330~350 萬顆，增長+21%YoY 。NVIDIA 體系
GPU，Rubin 系列層數提升至 24~26L，材料端從 M8/M8+往更高規
格升級，更高層數與多層式架構，促使 PCB 板訊號走線、供電完
整性、高速訊號之阻抗匹配控制、訊號傳輸完整性，皆帶動 PCB

### Page 1

HD현대중공업의 3Q26 연결 실적은 매출액 5조 8,602억원(yoy +33%, qoq -7%), 
영업이익 9,161억원(yoy +64%, qoq -12%, OPM 15.6%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 하회할 
전망이다. 동사의 3Q26 조업일수는 여름휴가, 추석연휴 및 중대재해 등 영향으로 
qoq -21% 감소했으며 환율 역시 qoq -84원 감소한 영향을 감안해 전분기 대비 
실적을 보수적으로 추정했다. 그럼에도 해양 부문 Trion FPU발 C/O에 따른 일회성 
환입이 예상됨에 따라 일부 이익 상쇄가 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 한편, 동사의 
임단협 부결로 당분기 추가적인 인건비 부담은 제한적일 것으로 판단되며 잠수함 
화재 사고 관련 비용 역시 4Q 이후 반영될 전망이다.

동사는 급격히 커져가는 AIDC향 전력수요 및 발전용 엔진 수주에 대응하고자 9/10 
총 4GW 규모의 4행정 중속엔진 캐파 증설을 위한 신규 투자를 공시헸다. 동사의 
기존 DC향 4행정 중속엔진 수주 단가를 고려 시 GW당 약 1조원의 매출 창출이 
가능할 전망이며 2행정 및 선박용 4행정 엔진 대비 높은 수익성이 예상되는 바 
생산이 본격화될 28년부터 동사 실적 성장성의 핵심 동인으로 작용할 전망이다.

동사에 대해 투자의견 BUY를 유지하며 업종 밸류에이션 디레이팅 및 시장 프리미엄 
조정에 따른 Target Multipe 변동으로 목표주가를 70만원으로 하향 조정한다. 상선 
시장 수주 경쟁 심화로 업종 밸류 조정이 이루어지고 있으나 동사는 엔진 기반 장기 
성장성으로 Peer 대비 상대적 매력도 확보 및 주가 방어가 가능하다는 판단이다.

3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후: ESS ↑, BEV ↓  
LG에너지솔루션의 3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후 배터리 섹터는 11/3 미국 중간선거
를 전후로 Trading Long 투자의견이 유효하다. 미국 중간선거 이후 특정정당 우세와 
별개로, 미국 Ex-China ESS 공급망 프리미엄은 유효하다. 동사 역시 그간의 ESS 
사업 난제(단위당 수율, 팩 병목)는 점진적 해소될 점이다. 반면 시장의 BEV 반등 기
대감에 동의하지 않는다. EU 프랑스와 독일의 내연기관 퇴출시점 지연 움직임 및 미
국 연비규제 정책 폐지 결정으로 전통 완성차 고객사들향 BEV 배터리 출하 부진은 
여전하겠다. 미국의 BEV 정책 변화시점은 28.11월 대선으로 지연될 전망이다.

ESS 모멘텀: 한국 제3차 ESS 중앙계약시장 사업자 선정 모멘텀 
대다수 시장 참여자들은 2026년 연말까지 LG에너지솔루션의 미국 ESS 60Gwh  규
모의 잔여 수주 달성 가시성에 집중한다. 해당 ESS 사업 수주 가이던스 관건은 시기
보다 방향성이다. 단, 당사가 주목하는 연말 모멘텀은 한국 정부향 제3차 ESS 중앙
계약시장 사업자 선정으로 판단한다. 3차 규모는 1.13Gw로 1&2차 계약 규모(합산 
1.13Gw) 대비 확대되었다. 또한 언론보도상 LG에너지솔루션은 해당 수주를 위한 핵
심 4대 소재 등 전 Value-chain 국산화를 선언한 바 있다. 해당 수주 확정시, 동사
를 비롯한 해당 Value-chain에 긍정적 모멘텀이 예상된다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 51만원 제시   
26년 연중 지정학 Risk, 에너지 대전환 영향 등에 전력 인프라 수요는 꾸준할 전망
이다. 향후 국내 이차전지 산업은 ‘L’ Shaped Cycle 전망, 반면 동사는 스마트카 T사
향 원통형 전지 출하량 증가, ESS 제조업 이상으로 변모하는 과정으로 산업 평균 성
장률 Outperform 가능하다. 투자의견 Buy 유지, 적정주가는 추정치 상향에 51만원
을 제시한다.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Recent revisions to US PCE inflation, UK productivity
and Canadian population estimates are reminders that
statistical measurement is imperfect. Revisions are both a
fact of life and, when they improve measurement, desirable.
• Australian data will also be revised over time, though

not necessarily in the same ways as these latest revisions
abroad. The ABS tends to favour quality over speed,
which reduces the need for early revisions as more data
become available. But revisions can still matter. Population
estimates implied in the initial releases of labour market and
GDP data are especially likely to be revised in the near term.
• Estimates of current trends can change as future data are
published. Shifting seasonal patterns are another source

of revision, especially for monthly CPI components with
short histories. Series that are derived from multiple other
imperfectly measured series are more prone to revision
than quantities that can be measured directly. All these
considerations point to a need for humility when using
recent data to make strong policy pronouncements.
On September 30, the Fed’s preferred measure of inflation,
PCE, was revised down. New methods and data sources were
applied to three components of services inflation (financial

advice, software and legal services), large enough to result in
downward revisions to the overall read on core PCE inflation.
These changes had been pre-announced in June and were
therefore not a surprise. The CPI measure of inflation was not
affected, nor should the revisions materially change your view
of US inflationary pressures.
Some revisions are more consequential for interpretation and
policy. A couple of weeks before the US PCE revision, it was

revealed that the UK Office for National Statistics was revising
down its estimates of average hours worked per worker. The
result was that measured productivity growth was nowhere
near as weak as had been widely believed. As my friend and
counterpart, TS Lombard’s Chief Economist Freya Beamish
commented on social media, “Oh well. Not like any major policy
decisions were taken on the basis of those numbers.” Quite.
Also in September, Statistics Canada revised up its estimates

of Canada’s population, taking the recent growth rates
from outright contraction to still-slow growth a little below
½%yr. The number of temporary residents in Canada is still
falling, but the revised data do a better job of tracking them,
especially those seeking an extension on their permit. (An alert
When reality gets revised
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
for customers who have seen the cross-country population
graph I often use at client events: the Canadian line now looks

different but still shows a sharp slowing.)
There is no fault to be attributed here. Revisions to key
statistics are a part of economic life. Sometimes the underlying
data arrive with a lag, perhaps because of reporting deadlines.
Sometimes the compiling agency improves its methods or
gains access to a better data source. The US and UK examples
above fall into this category. In the Canadian case, better
data and a more accurate definition of scope were involved.

All these revisions should be welcomed. They provide a more
accurate picture of the underlying reality.
Australia, revised
Revisions are a fact of life for Australian statistics, too. I am
still a little scarred by that unexpected –0.5%qtr GDP print
when the September quarter 2016 data were first released on
my first day as RBA Assistant Governor; the figure has since
been revised to 0%qtr and was followed by a big bounce-back
in the December quarter.

The recent revisions mentioned above are less likely to be
replicated in Australia, though. Australia’s CPI is designed such
that the headline (not seasonally adjusted) measure is never
revised. This is because it is used in many legal contracts.
If an error is detected, the ABS makes the adjustment in
subsequent months and quarters rather than revising the
history. Our labour force survey has a much higher response
rate than its UK equivalent – Australians are a compliant lot

when the ABS comes knocking – and measures actual hours
worked more reliably. And our population statistics already
handle the array of temporary resident visas with the Bureau
instead applying a standard 12-month-in-16-month residency
test to count as a resident for the population statistics rather
than using a visa-classification basis.
This last case illustrates Australia’s broader tendency to
favour quality over speed in official statistics. Our CPI and

national accounts data tend to be published a bit later than
peer countries’ data for the same reference period, and our
“Economic data are often revised. Sometimes
the revisions change the story; sometimes
they do not."

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
estimated resident population data is quite a bit lagged
relative to Canada’s (the 12-month-in-16 criteria taking over
a year to confirm). The trade-off is that observers of the
Australian economy must wait longer but can be less worried
about “flash” and “advance” releases of data that can be

quickly revised to tell a completely different story.
Revisions to data still can matter in Australia, and it helps
to be aware of the specific cases where it might occur. For
example, precisely because the population data are very
lagged, the population estimates underlying the initial
releases of the labour force survey and national accounts are
based on preliminary data and get revised later as a matter
of routine. This is why ratios such as the unemployment,

employment-to-population and participation rates provide a
better real-time gauge of labour market conditions than the
monthly change in employment. Similarly, GDP per capita can
be revised even when GDP is not. This type of revision is also
a more likely source of short-term changes in measured hours
worked in the Australian productivity data than the conceptual
change that shifted the UK data.
The usual reporting lags – and occasional correction of

reporting errors by the reporting entity – will always be a factor
in Australia just as it is in other countries. The annual national
accounts, due on 23 October, incorporate additional data, revise
the quarterly estimates and provide first estimates of important
quantities such as the capital stock. Just as importantly, though,
the annual accounts reconcile the different ways of measuring
GDP. In doing so, what we thought we knew about the recent

history of output growth can change.
Every purchase is also a sale, so conceptually, you should get the
same answer for final output regardless of whether you count
how much is produced, how much people spent on it, or how
much income people got for producing it. In practice, though,
the “production”, “expenditure” and “income” approaches do
not always line up until the ABS goes through a more thorough
reconciliation process in compiling the annual accounts.

When the future changes the past
Future data can also change our view of the past. Two good
examples are trend estimation and seasonal adjustment. Future
observations help reveal whether an apparent trend is stable.
As a result, real-time trend estimates can differ considerably
from those calculated with the benefit of later data.
Identifying stable seasonal patterns also requires a reasonable
run of data. Future observations affect whether an earlier

movement is judged to be normal seasonal variation. This
problem is more pronounced for newer series, where there is
not yet enough history to establish what “normal” looks like.
Consider, then, the monthly CPI. This relatively new measure has
only a short run of data with many individual components having
too little history to use standard seasonal adjustment methods.
As Westpac’s senior economist Justin Smirk has previously
highlighted, some components such as footwear are being

‘seasonally adjusted’ by applying Henderson trends, similar
to those used in the ‘trend’ measures of labour market and
national accounts data. For some other components, including
books, furniture, audio-visual and computing equipment and
children’s clothes, certain price falls – usually associated with
sales periods – are just arbitrarily removed from the seasonally
adjusted series. The index is held at its previous month’s level
but otherwise matches the non-seasonally adjusted series.

The seasonally adjusted measures of these CPI components will
inevitably be revised over time, and thus so could the monthly
trimmed mean estimates. This helps explain why the RBA has
said it would, for now, focus on the quarterly trimmed mean
based on the old seasonal factors. That measure will at least
not be buffeted by shifting measurement of seasonality in a
short history. If seasonal patterns are themselves also shifting,
the current underlying trend might look a bit different in a few

years, not just a smoother month-to-month profile.
The broader point here is to remember that economic statistics
are a lens on reality. Sometimes the lens is smudged, sometimes
the scope is off and sometimes what is being read as underlying
trends is wrong. Measures constructed from several imperfect
series – productivity from output and hours worked, or unit labour
costs from productivity and labour costs, or savings rates derived
from income and spending – are especially prone to revision

compared with concepts that can be measured more directly, like
a price. That is a strong argument for humility before making firm
policy pronouncements based on simple data comparisons.

THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: mulling the minutes
consistent with a labour market that is gradually normalising
than one entering a meaningful downturn.
The September FOMC minutes reinforced a distinctly hawkish
message, with participants noting they had "not seen

sufficient progress on lowering inflation in recent months" and
expressing concern that inflation risks remained "skewed to the
upside". Policymakers highlighted that both core services and
core goods inflation remained elevated, with some attributing
persistent goods price strength to the effects of the AI buildout
despite the sector's traditional disinflationary role. There was
also concern that, after more than five years of inflation above
target, elevated inflation could begin to influence inflation

expectations and wage- and price-setting behaviour. In
contrast, labour market conditions were generally viewed as
stable and "close to maximum employment". While a majority
noted the labour market had strengthened somewhat recently,
several participants highlighted unusually low dynamism,
characterised by low hiring, low layoffs, a low job-finding
rate and elevated long-term unemployment. Importantly,
participants generally judged that aggregate wage growth

was moderate and "consistent with inflation moving toward 2
percent", with several explicitly noting that "the labor market
was not currently a source of inflationary pressures".
While participants broadly expected AI-related investment to
boost productivity growth and potential output over time, several
warned that "the AI buildout could cause aggregate demand
to outpace aggregate supply over the medium term, putting
upward pressure on inflation". Consistent with this view, some

participants had raised their estimate of the neutral policy rate
and several judged the current policy stance to be "not restrictive
or only mildly restrictive". Against this backdrop, all participants
supported the September rate increase, and most assessed
that another increase in the federal funds rate would likely be
appropriate by year-end, although future decisions would remain
data dependent.
The September ISM Services PMI released this week painted

a similar picture of an economy that remains resilient but is
gradually losing momentum. The headline index eased to 54.9
from 55.4 in August, with the decline driven by a sharp fall in
business activity and a 9.4pt drop in new export orders, which
slipped into contractionary territory for the first time in eight
The only data release of note in Australia this week was
the October Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment Survey ,
which reported a 4.7% drop in the headline index. At

80.4, sentiment has tumbled back to deeply pessimistic
levels, similar to the post-pandemic cost-of-living shock
when readings were among the weakest in the survey’s
more than fifty-year history. Indeed, inflation and interest
rate pressures are once again clearly front of mind for
consumers, with both the view on ‘family finances vs a
year ago’ and ‘family finance next 12 months’ falling 8.0%
and 6.4% in the month, respectively. Moreover, the RBA’s

September rate hike looks to have had a significant impact –
sentiment tanked 20% between those surveyed before the
RBA decision and those surveyed afterwards.
Views on the economic outlook were relatively more resilient,
but they still weakened, with the one-year and five-year ahead
sub-indexes falling 1.6% and 0.4% respectively. Consumers
are becoming more ‘on edge’ about job security, albeit not
outright alarmed. The unemployment rate remains relatively

low versus history, although it has drifted up to a cycle high
of 4.6% as labour supply continues to outstrip demand. We
expect it to hold there in next week’s September Labour Force
Survey – see our preview for more detail. All told, with higher
interest rates, renewed inflation pressures and a softening
jobs market all weighing at once, households are entering the
final months of the year on the back foot.
Offshore, the focus was on US data.
Before turning to this week's releases, it is worth noting that

September non-farm payrolls came in softer than expected
across most metrics. Employment rose by only 29k (89k
expected), and there were sizeable downward revisions
to prior months, with a combined 60k removed from July
and August payrolls, leaving gains of just 31k and 29k
respectively. The details pointed to weaker hiring in leisure
& hospitality and education & health services industries,
although construction and manufacturing employment
continued to benefit from ongoing data-centre-related

investment. The unemployment rate edged up to 4.2%, with
softer employment occurring alongside a rise in labour force
participation to 61.8%. Meanwhile wage growth moderated
to 0.1%mth (3.0%yr). Taken together, the report suggests
labour demand is cooling, with the three-month average
pace of job creation slowing to around 50k. However, softer
payroll gains are not necessarily indicative of labour market
deterioration. Lower immigration and ageing demographics

have reduced the pace of employment growth needed to keep
the unemployment rate stable. As such, the report is more
Sian Fenner, Head of Business and Industry Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist
“A key focus was the interaction between
AI investment, productive capacity and
inflation."

months. Labour market conditions improved modestly with
the employment index moving to broadly neutral territory
50.1 after two months below 50, supported by elevated

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Fuel, housing and the bumpy road ahead
Higher fuel costs, a weaker exchange rate, and a softer
housing market have prompted a refresh of our near-term
forecasts. Inflation looks set to finish 2026 above 4%, while
growth could lose a little momentum into year-end. Even so,

we continue to expect the RBNZ to pause in October before
lifting the OCR to 3% in December.
New Zealand’s recovery is still moving forward, but the road
has become rougher. Since our last forecast update in August,
global oil and refined-fuel prices have climbed sharply, the
New Zealand dollar has fallen, and longer-term interest rates
have moved higher. At home, the housing market has remained
weaker than we anticipated. None of those developments,

on their own, are enough to derail the recovery. Together,
however, they point to a tougher finish to 2026 for households
and businesses. We have therefore revisited our near-term
inflation, housing and growth forecasts. The broad story
remains one of continued economic expansion and gradually
improving labour demand, but with a more pronounced
inflation spike and slightly less momentum in consumer
spending. Our OCR call is unchanged: a pause at the October

review, followed by a 25 basis point increase to 3% at the
December Monetary Policy Statement.
A temporary inflation reprieve
We continue to think that consumer prices rose 0.7% in the
September quarter. That would lower annual inflation from
4.1% in June to 3.7% in September, broadly consistent with
the monthly price updates published so far. The final monthly
release for the quarter is due on 16 October and will help firm up
that estimate. But any easing in the annual rate is likely to prove

short-lived. We now expect prices to rise 1.1% in the December
quarter, lifting annual inflation to a three-year high of 4.2% by
year-end. That is half a percentage point above our previous
projection and above the RBNZ’s September forecast of 3.9%.
The reason is easy to see at the petrol pump. Higher global
fuel prices have been amplified by a more than 4% fall in
the New Zealand dollar since the start of September. Local
petrol prices have risen by around 50 cents a litre over the

past month, with the nationwide average price of 91 unleaded
reaching a record $3.55 a litre this week. RBNZ Governor
Breman acknowledged a couple of weeks ago that higher
oil prices have tilted the risks around the near-term inflation
outlook to the upside. Importantly, however, the RBNZ remains
focused on the medium-term inflation outlook rather than
trying to offset every movement in volatile fuel prices.
For now, we have left our quarterly inflation forecasts for 2027

unchanged. Even so, the higher starting point at the end of this
year means annual inflation is likely to remain firm through much
of 2027. Our updated profile has inflation at 3.7% in March
2027, 2.6% in June, 2.5% in September and 1.7% in December.
That is broadly similar to the RBNZ’s path through the first three
quarters, although our forecast falls more quickly late in the
year. We will take a fuller look at the 2027 inflation and growth
outlook next week alongside the monthly Market Outlook.

Housing fails to find its feet
The housing market has also delivered a disappointment. We
had expected prices to stabilise through the second half of
2026, but sales volumes have continued to weaken, and the
median time required to sell a property has lengthened to 48
days. Average prices have kept edging lower, despite firmer
conditions in parts of the South Island with greater exposure
to the strong export economy. The latest indicators do not
suggest that a decisive turn is close. We have therefore revised

our forecast for national house price growth in 2026 from a
0.6% rise to a 1.4% decline. That incorporates quarterly falls
of 0.5% in September and 0.8% in December.
The headwinds are familiar: the labour market remains soft,
mortgage rates are higher and employment growth is still
moderate. Those forces are likely to keep the market subdued
for some time, with risks tilted towards another weak year
in 2027. Changes to the taxation of capital gains on investor

housing following the 2026 General Election may add another
potential drag.
Kelly Eckhold
Chief Economist NZ

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Regional outcomes will continue to differ. Tourism- and
agriculture-focused areas should benefit from stronger income
and employment growth, supported by robust commodity export
prices. We have lifted our 2026/27 Fonterra Milk Price forecast to
$9.75/kg and set an opening forecast of $9.50/kg for 2027/28. In

contrast, homeowners in the major North Island cities may have to
wait longer for the wealth boost that comes with rising property
values. Data released this week shows that household wealth
continues to be dragged down by declining housing values.
A little less momentum
The weaker housing backdrop matters for the wider economy.
When petrol absorbs more of the household budget and home
values are drifting lower, consumers tend to become more
cautious. That combination is likely to crimp discretionary

spending in the run-up to Christmas and could take some
steam out of the construction recovery. That’s even though our
own card spending data for September  showed resilience in
consumer spending. Recent business and consumer confidence
surveys have nevertheless remained resilient despite the rise
in energy prices and long-term interest rates. Our short-term
growth forecasts therefore remain broadly intact, but we have
trimmed our December quarter consumer spending projection

and now expect GDP growth of 2.5% over 2026. That is still
a solid result, although the balance of risks has shifted to
the downside.
There are several moving parts to watch. Sustained high fuel
prices would squeeze household purchasing power more than
we have allowed for. The sharp lift in long-term interest rates
may also weigh on activity through 2027, even if shorter-
term borrowing costs evolve as expected. The election result
introduces two-sided risks that are difficult to quantify before

the shape and timing of policy changes are known. Against that,
strong agricultural incomes and signs of improving employment
growth should provide a buffer. We have not changed our 2026
labour market forecasts. Hiring appears to be strengthening
gradually, but it will take time before that improvement
translates into a noticeable fall in the unemployment rate.
The RBNZ should still pause before Christmas
Despite the higher near-term inflation track, we are not

changing our OCR forecast. Since the July Monetary Policy
Review, our view has been that the RBNZ would slow the pace
of increases as the OCR approached the Bank’s estimate of a
neutral 3%. The September meeting clearly signalled a pause
in October and one further rise in December. We continue to
regard that as the most likely path. The latest developments
raise headline inflation but, so far, there is little evidence that
inflation expectations are moving higher or that the fuel shock

is generating broader, persistent price pressure. At the same
time, growth risks warrant some caution.
A pause in October would give the Monetary Policy Committee
time to assess the cumulative effect of the increases already
delivered and to refine its strategy for 2027. Financial
conditions have also tightened from an unexpected source:
longer-term interest rates have risen markedly over the past
six weeks. That increase has at least offset the easing impulse

from the lower exchange rate, even though the weaker
currency is adding to imported inflation in the near term. We
still think the RBNZ is likely to have more work to do in 2027,
but there is plenty of data to come before that judgement
needs to be made.
For now, the best description of the outlook is resilient but
less comfortable. Export incomes remain strong, activity is
expanding and the labour market is beginning to improve. But
households face an awkward combination of record petrol

prices, soft home values and higher borrowing costs. That
mix points to a bumpy end to the year: inflation will be hotter,
spending growth a little cooler and the recovery more uneven
across regions. The RBNZ can afford to wait in October, but if
the economy develops broadly as we expect, a further hike to
3% in December will still be needed.

AUS: Sep Labour Force – Employment Change (000s)
Oct 15, Last: 39.5, Westpac f/c: 20 
Market f/c: 20, Range: flat to 35
Employment surprised to the upside in August with a 39k gain. 
Looking through the monthly swings, while momentum has 
improved over recent months, employment growth has largely 
continued to bump around below its historic average and the 
current pace of working age population growth.
Our preview provides a deeper look into the various gauges 
of labour demand – the LFS suggests employment growth 
is sluggish but broader measures in the Q2 Labour Account 
showed a stronger narrative. Meanwhile job ads and business 
surveys are also pulling in different directions.
For September, we have pencilled in a lift in employment of 
+20k, which would see annual growth hold in recent ranges.
AUS: Sep Labour Force – Unemployment Rate (%)
Oct 15, Last: 4.6, Westpac f/c: 4.6 
Market f/c: 4.6, Range: 4.5 to 4.7
The participation rate jumped to 67.1% in August, just under 
the 115-year high recorded in January 2025. With labour 
supply once again outstripping demand, the unemployment 
rate rose from 4.5% to 4.6% – the highest since in late-2021, 
close to rounding up to 4.7% (4.646% at three decimal places).
A softer economy and sub-par employment growth typically 
discourages some people to search for work, but cost-of-living 
pressures and interest rate rises are more than outweighing this 
effect, encouraging more people into the labour market.
With the participation rate expected to pare back to 67.0%, we 
think the unemployment rate will hold steady at 4.6%. Please 
see our preview for more detail.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Sep REINZ House Sales and Prices (%ann)
Oct 14, Sales Last: –13.0, Prices Last: –0.9
The housing market has remained soft in recent months,
with sales falling to a two-year low and prices edging lower.
Mortgage rates have risen as the RBNZ has initiated a new

tightening cycle, and a growing supply of unsold homes on the
market means that buyers are under no pressure to bid prices
higher. Conditions remain stronger in the south, with prices
up on last year in Canterbury, Otago and Southland, while
Auckland and Wellington are the weakest markets.
NZ: Sep Retail Card Spending (%mth)
Oct 16, Last: –0.9, Westpac f/c: 0.8
We’re forecasting a 0.8% rise in retail spending in September,
reversing the 0.9% fall seen in the previous month. In part, the

forecast rise in spending is due to the sharp increases in petrol
prices in recent weeks. Those higher fuel costs are weighing on
spending in other categories like hospitality. Nevertheless, we
still expect a 0.5% rise in core (ex-fuel) spending. Stats NZ’s
monthly retail spending figures have been very choppy for the
past two years, and we’re likely to see a bounce following last
month’s unexpected falls in categories like furnishings.
NZ: Sep Selected Consumer Prices (%mth)

Oct 16
This will be the last monthly update ahead of the full quarterly
CPI release on 22 October. We’re currently forecasting a 0.7%
quarterly rise in the CPI (+3.7%yr) and will be watching the
detail of the Selected Prices update to pin down that forecast.
In terms of the big categories, we’re forecasting a roughly 6%
rise in petrol costs, along with a 0.7% rise in food prices. That’s
balanced against continued softness in housing rents (which
are the single largest component of the CPI). The key sources

of risk for our forecast are the volatile travel categories, most
of which recorded sizeable drops last month that we expect to
partially reverse in September.

[Nhận định xu hướng] 
VN-Index có thể hồi kỹ thuật từ cạnh dưới tam giác, song việc chớm thủng hỗ trợ 
1.710 điểm và số mã còn giữ xu hướng tăng từ đáy tháng 7 suy gi ảm khiến xu 
hướng ngắn hạn trở nên trung tính.

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -29.18 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Dầu khí, Bảo hiểm và Dịch vụ phân phối là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -3,013 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã MCH, VIC và FPT; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở mức 
887 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -2,021 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -4,384 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng -
4,275 hợp đồng và đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -7,815 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam tiếp tục điều chỉnh tuần thứ tư liên tiếp, khi VN-Index lùi về đóng cửa 1.706 
điểm - giảm khoảng 1,7% so với tuần trước và đang dao động quanh vùng hỗ trợ tâm lý 
1.700 điểm, cũng là vùng đáy cũ của tháng 8. Thanh khoản thị trường tiếp tục suy yếu, 
phản ánh tâm lý thận trọng cao độ trong bối cảnh thị trường thiếu vắng thông tin hỗ trợ 
trong nước, trong khi các yếu tố bên ngoài như xung đột tại Iran, và chi phí vốn toàn cầu 
chưa hạ nhiệt tiếp tục đè nặng tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Khối ngoại duy trì chuỗi bán ròng kéo 
dài nhiều phiên liên tiếp, nối dài đà rút vốn đã lên tới xấp xỉ 95 nghìn tỷ đồng từ đầu năm. 
Trong một, hai tuần tới, mùa báo cáo kết quả kinh doanh quý 3 được kỳ vọng là chất xúc 
tác giúp thị trường giao dịch cân bằng trở lại.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tiếp tục hình thành mẫu nến giảm điểm tiêu cực trên đồ thị tuần, đánh dấu tuần 
giảm thứ 4 liên tiếp, cho thấy áp lực phân phối vẫn đang có phần lấn át. Trên khung ngày, 
tuy chỉ số đã có nỗ lực phục hồi trở lại trong 3 phiên đan xen, đặc biệt là phản ứng trong 
phiên cuối tuần khi chạm trendline trung hạn, nhưng việc chỉ có thể hình thành các mẫu 
nến "spinning" hàm ý đà tích lũy chưa thực sự thuyết phục. Trong một vài phiên kế tiếp, 
VNINDEX có thể xuất hiện nhịp hồi kỹ thuật từ vùng hỗ trợ, tương ứng với cạnh dưới tam 
giác, nhưng sau khi chớm phá vùng hỗ trợ quanh 1710 và hiện chỉ còn một số ít các mã giữ 
được xu hướng tăng tính từ đáy tháng 7, xu hướng ngắn hạn đang trở nên khá trung tính.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1780 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1740 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1680 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1630 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Document 341: India_ Degrees of rupee defence (2026-10-16)

### Page 1

TAKING STOCK

The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) has adopted a 
multi-layered defence strategy to support the 
rupee this year.

This has entailed a combination of outright 
intervention in spot/ forwards, regulatory 
action, liquidity steps, administrative measures 
and moves to curb import -related dollar 
demand.

The rupee has appreciated by 1.8% against the 
dollar in the past five days since recent 
measures took effect, paring YTD depreciation 
to 5.5% . High oil prices, weakness in capital 
inflows, a firm dollar , tariffs/ geopolitical 
worries, energy , and risk -off sentiments have 
kept the INR under pressure since last year.

layered defence strategy to

although the circumstances 
differ.

accompanied with distortions 
elsewhere.

and remain ready to take

• If FX depreciation pressures 
return, a  larger scale of hike 
or inter-meeting move might 
also be considered.

India: Degrees of rupee defence

16 October 2026 
Radhika Rao, Senior Economist 
radhikarao@dbs.com

### Page 2

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

ECHOES OF 2013 WITH FEW 
DIFFERENCES

Comparisons with 2013 are apt, though 
the backdrop differs.  As for the 
catalysts, markets reacted to the US 
Fed’s taper announcement in 2013, 
which pushed US yields higher  and led 
global investors to reduce exposure to 
vulnerable EM economies. As a result, 
capital inflows weakened, exposing 
funding mismatch es for countries, 
including India, that were running wide 
current account deficits.

In 2026, markets face the spectre of 
elevated oil prices, geopolitical 
uncertainty, global competition for 
capital, a firm dollar ( DXY appreciated 
+3.8% YTD) , and US inflation/ deficits 
which have settled at levels much higher 
than pre -pandemic years. This has 
pushed up UST yields, and the US Fed 
kicked off its rate-hike cycle in Sep26.

Against this backdrop, the RBI is 
pursuing the same objective , i.e., to 
ensure rupee stability, but the nature of 
the battle differs. The defence strategy 
has shifted from ‘crisis management’ 
mode in 2013 , due to weaker domestic 
macroeconomic balances (a wide current 
account gap, high inflation, a lower 
reserve buffer, and wide fiscal deficits) , 
to a ‘terms-of-trade shock and impact of 
a firm dollar’ in 2026.

Flashpoints are  nonetheless similar, 
with reliance on portfolio financing , 
leaving the external account vulnerable 
to shifts in global risk sentiment. 
Portfolio outflows have emerged as the 
primary transmission channel through 
which external shocks have translated 
into INR depreciation pressure , 
compounded by high energy prices and a 
firm dollar.

FX PLAYBOOK

While the response framework is similar 
between 2013 and this year, the broad 
sequence and nature of macroprudential 
measures differ. This year’s multi-
layered rupee defence toolkit has 
included:

-Direct FX defence  by way of 
intervention in the spot and forwards 
market. As of Aug26, the net forward 
dollar liabilities ballooned to $200bn, 
rising by ~$63bn vs . the month before . 
Recent sell/buy FX swaps are expected 
to lower this stock in the coming months.

-Attracting dollar inflows  by way of 
special swap facilities for FCNR deposits, 
and offshore borrowings , apart from 
liberalising taxes around debt inflows,

### Page 3

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

amongst others. We discussed this here, 
here, here and here.

Taking a proactive stance, a portion of 
existing FX reserves could be earmarked 
against forward liabilities (from maturing 
FCNR deposits) , helping to mitigate 
concerns that deposit maturities or debt 
repayments could trigger a sharp
increase in dollar ⁠demand down the line.

-Domestic liquidity management. Bonds, 
to some degree, are paying the price for 
the authorities' efforts to defend the 
rupee against the additional headwind of 
weak global conditions.  INR liquidity 
surged on the back of the swap windows, 
prompting the RBI to undertake open 
market operations, VRRR auctions, sell -
buy swaps, among others. While these 
have been effective in absorbing 
balances, bond yields have drifted higher. 
10Y yields are at three-year highs and are 
expected to climb further  (DBSf: 7.40 -
7.50% by year-end).

-Moderate FX demand and speculation . 
Earlier in the year, the RBI had 
introduced change s to the banks’ net 
open position framework, and thereafter 
partially rolled back a few curbs. 
Prudential measures on FX exposures 
and hedging -related measures were 
announced in Oct26, which include d the 
introduction of a daily cash reserve with 
the RBI equal to 20% of the rupee 
equivalent of the notional value for INR -
linked derivative contracts exceeding 
$2mn used to hedge current account 
transactions, referred to as the Foreign 
Exchange Risk Reserve (FERR) . 
Subsequent clarifications provided 
operational details on where the FERR 
would be maintained and that it would be 
subject to audits.

Since 12 Oct, the RBI has been providing 
dollars via designated banks to the three 
state-owned oil -marketing companies 
(Indian Oil, BPCL, and HPCL) to meet the 
their daily dollar requirements, thereby 
diverting related spot-market demand.

On hedging-related matters, the limit for 
undertaking foreign exchange derivative 
transactions without proving an 
underlying commercial exposure was 
cut to $5mn from $100mn across all ADs 
and exchange -traded currency 
derivatives. Restrictions on Rebooking , 
i.e., a uthorized dealers are prohibited 
from allowing customers to rebook 
cancelled rupee -linked derivative 
contracts (though rollovers upon 
maturity remain permitted). Lastly, 
banks must obtain written confirmation 
from users that the same underlying 
exposure has not been hedged with 
another dealer.  Data from CCI , cited by

### Page 4

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

Bloomberg, pointed to importers’ 
forward dollar purchases rising 44% on 
the year to a monthly average of $64bn.

-Current account management . Efforts 
to contain structural FX outflows by way 
of higher duties and taxes on gold 
imports discourage non -essential 
purchases and production incentives to 
improve export competitiveness.

IMPACT

To assess whether the playbook has 
worked, we observe:

a) Rupee has enjoyed a brief respite. 
Despite oil prices at $105/bbl at the time 
of writing and elevated global yields, 
USDINR fell from near 96.80 to wards 
95.00, owing to measures announced in 
Oct26 following the central bank’s 25bp 
hike. Beyond temporary relief, global 
catalysts are likely to be back to the fore 
and weigh on the currency (see chart);

b) Sharp inflows under the swap 
facilities pushed the foreign reserves 
stock to a record high, providing 
policymakers sufficient buffer to 
navigate future shocks from a position of 
strength. A strong interventionist 
response has since led to a softening in 
the overall stock (see chart).

c) Macroprudential measures have led to 
distortions in other parts of the market . 
For instance, strong swap -related 
inflows had inorganically pushed 
onshore INR liquidity up sharply, 
necessitating absorption measures. 
Similarly, NOP restrictions and the more 
recent tighter requirements on hedging 
activity and counterparty transactions  
had some after -effects. Forward points 
have surged this week , with financial 
institutions pointing to relative illiquidity 
in the currency forwards market  on 
volumes and activity basis, and fewer 
avenues to deal with dollar supply.

### Page 5

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

d) Swap-related windows are a timely 
support for the country’s balance of 
payments position. From expectations of 
a third consecutive year of BOP deficit in 
FY27, the math has now swung to a 
potential surplus, even as current 
account worries remain. With energy 
prices settling north of $100/bbl, 
keeping the monthly trade deficit near 
$30bn, our FY27 current account deficit 
forecast of 1.1% of GDP faces  upside 
risks of around 0.3-0.5% of GDP. We note 
the s harp jump in ECB outflows in S ep, 
though see that as a temporary overshot 
of leverage-FCNR activity.

WHAT LIES AHEAD

Authorities are likely not done yet and 
remain ready to take further action, as 
required. RBI Governor Malhotra 
reiterated the authorities’ serious intent 
of stabilising the currency in a recent 
interview (Moneycontrol, 13 Oct), 
emphasising that “The RBI is determined 
to do whatever is necessary to ensure 
orderly movement in the foreign 
exchange market,”. He also highlighted 
that recent steps were not tantamount to 
capital control measures that were 
aimed at ensuring that forex markets 
serve genuine hedging needs rather than 
speculative bets.  Market participants 
opine that FX volumes and interest are 
likely to be subdued in a few pockets on 
the back of the recent measures, 
consequently stabilizing the rupee.

Unlike in 2013, when policy tightening 
was a part of the initial response, the 
2026 strategy has prioritised targeted 
FX and regulatory measures.  The 
sequencing suggests a deliberate effort 
to distinguish actions aimed at 
preserving currency stability from those 
intended to achieve domestic monetary 
objectives, with rates remaining a 
potential, though not immediate, tool in 
the defence framework.

However, global developments have 
altered that dynamic . Rising US yields 
and a gradual increase in the US Fed 
Funds rate (DBS baseline: a total of 75bp 
hikes) have compressed IN -US spreads. 
The recent sell -off in INR benchmark

### Page 6

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

bonds has effectively widened Gsec -
UST spreads across maturities, in 
contrast to the compression witnessed in 
regional high-yielders.

The latest announcements may not 
fully quell speculation over further 
policy action.

Upcoming measures might include -

-Markets anticipate relief on the long -
term capital gains tax structure on 
equities to draw in longer-duration 
inflows. This measure will boost inflows 
if it coincides with other factors that 
make domestic equities attractive for 
thematic investing , as global risk -free 
rates moderate.

-Street expectations also include steps 
to boost inward dollar repatriation by 
exporters to boost domestic dollar 
supply.

-Wider policy rate corridor akin to 2013.

-Policy rate response. While this might 
not be a panacea, it is a likely necessary 
condition. Raising the neutral rate might 
have mixed success in attracting capital 
market inflows ; though rate-sensitive 
flows are likely to get a fillip as investors 
seek attractive rate differentials in the 
region.

An adequate real rate cushion would 
also align with the evolving domestic 
inflation realities, which are expected to 
accelerate to 6% in the Oct-Dec quarter 
and remain above 5% well into 2027.

Given fluid global developments, we 
outline three scenarios on the rate path 
ahead. Our baseline assumption  is for 
75bp hikes in this cycle  (including Oct), 
spread between the Dece mber and 
February 2027 meetings. We don’t rule 
out an intermeeting rate hike before the 
December meeting, contingent on 
rupee movements.

Under the second scenario , s hould 
depreciation pressures on the rupee 
return, the 50bp hike might be brought 
forward to December , with another in 
February, taking cumulative hikes in this 
cycle to 100bp. The t hird scenario  is a 
more aggressive 150bp worth of hikes in 
this cycle, with 50bp front -loaded in 
December, and the rest spilling into 2027. 
Factors that  will determine whether 
these scenarios materialise are the 
resumption and scale of rupee 
depreciation, oil price direction, US Fed 
Funds rate increases (DBSf: total 75bps) 
and other risk-off events.

Path ahead

Despite these measures, we see limited 
room for sustained INR appreciation on 
grounds of manufacturing/ trade 
competitiveness. A challenging flows 
portfolio flows outlook leaves the d oor 
open to further gradual rupee 
depreciation subject to global 
developments, with high sensitivity to oil 
price movements and risk sentiments . A 
strong rate response will help introduce  
favourable rate differentials and reduce 
currency volatility.

Key takeaways
2ECONOMIC RESEARCH
1 A rise in inflation still confined to energy
Rising energy prices are pushing
inflation higher in Europe and the
United States, while energy’s
contribution is slightly negative in
Japan.
3 Mounting pressure on producer prices
2 Some recent pressure on core measures
4 Rising short-termmarket expectations
Markets (swaps) are pricing this
rise in inflation into short-term
measures. Long-term expectations
(5y5y) are rising in Japan and the

UK.
Over recent months (3m/3m), core
indices have been firming in the
euro area and Japan: a signal to
watch.
In Europe and Japan, pressure on
producer prices is building. The
risk of the energy shock spilling
over to other CPI items is
increasing.

美元，疊加生產者物價年 增速從前月4.8%升
至5.4%，凸顯短期通膨壓力難消 ，市場預期
聯準會9月升息機率升至 7成之上，打擊美股
走弱，僅個股利多消息推動費半指數漲勢相
對較 佳。 中東 衝 突 持續 造 成 市場對通 膨擔
憂，歐洲央行如預期升息一碼 ，日本銀行下

國消費者物價，為聯準會貨幣政策關鍵。

AI資料中心持續投資 ，正推動記憶體產業在
半導體產業占比大幅攀升 ，帶動美光等記憶
體大廠股價 上漲；英特爾 宣布CPU產品漲價
10%；甲骨文財報營收與獲利優於預期 ，亦
維持強勁展望； Meta 平台新推出 AI 助理
Muse，可為使用者寄電子郵件 、網購與規畫
行事曆等各種任務 ，科技與通訊類股漲幅居
前。美伊衝突仍未難消退 ，再度點燃油價漲
勢，能源類股表現相對抗跌。

財政部長施壓日本升息預期 ，日圓較主要貨
幣顯強，未來留意主要央行政策動向。

► ECB於9月10日把三項政策利率各上調 25個
基點，存款利率將於9月16日升至2.50%。央
行指出中東 衝突持續 製造通膨壓力，同時警
告增長下行風險 。ECB升息後，市場已訂價
明年4月升息超過75基點。
► 日 銀 下 周9 月17 至18 日 開 會， 委 員 高 田 創
(Hajime Takata)警告，在金融條件仍寬鬆 、
日圓偏弱及能源與食品成本上升下 ，若基礎
通膨加速 ，央行或須較 快升息；他認為現時
1%的政策利率仍然偏低 。不過，他亦表示通
膨未有急升，故毋須一次加50個基點行動。
► 全球面對能源供給衝擊推高 通膨，利率上升
影響收入和需求 。ECB已選擇先行抑制第二
輪通膨效應 ，代價是融資成本及成長股折現
壓力； 日銀 若 確 認 升息， 則進一 步支 持日
圓、推高日本公債 收益，加速日圓套利交易
平倉。日圓轉 強的情況下 ，我們預期銀行及
精選保險機構 、公用事業 、進口主導零售／
必需消費品、本土電信與服務業或能受惠。

投資焦點美國公債
► 美國財政部 9月10日執行10年期與20年期公
債回購計畫，並擴大回購規模從20億增至60
億美元 ，欲對缺乏 流動性的長天期舊債進行
流動性支持 ，減輕公債拋售 潮引發期限 溢價
走升問題 ，然實際回購規模僅 51.8億美元並
低於預期的 60億至100億美元令市場失望 。
財政部亦公布例行 10年與30年期公債標售結
果，宣布執行擴大回購操作後 ，從投標倍數
與得標 減預期利率 差距來看 ，反映長 天期公
債需求不淡 ，然川普承諾 共和黨若在期中選
舉中獲勝 ，將向全國成年 公民發錢，恐導致
未來公債 供給量續升 ，後續視 美國政府能否

維持財政紀律逐步恢復市場信心。
► 美國非農新增就業數明顯較前月改善 ，反映
就業市 場韌 性 ， 體現 實體 經濟表 現優 於 預
期，然短期 通膨難 回政策目標，預期聯準會
最快9月重啟升息 ，2年期公債利率水準已反
映升息空間 ，考量財政問題 與美國升 息預期
同在 ，投資美國公債宜慎 ，留意Fed官員態
度與經濟預估變化。
對等關稅宣布後，內需防禦股獲利下修幅度最少 美國經濟未見明顯疲態！為聯準會埋下升息伏筆財政部倍增長期公債回購不如預期，然標售需求回溫
美財政部倍增公債回購不如預期，保守看待公債配置

投資焦點
► 2026年第2季美國標普500企業財報數據顯示
獲利成長明顯高於債務增速 ，另可發現代表
獲利護城河的大型企業毛利率貼近 2022年以
來高位，淨利率亦創1998年以來新高 ，資本
支出年增至 25%歷史高位水準 ，另資本 支出
佔EBITDA比率升至 35%，預期美國大型企
業持續擴大資本 支出推動獲利 成長動能 ，同
時維持獲利高於資本開支的資本效率 ，避免
大額資本支出過度失控 ，造成未來債信體質
與償債能力惡化。
► 從今年1月至8月美國各天期投資級債表現來
看，可以發現中短天期債含息總報酬仍維持
正值，主因美國政府財政 問題與升息擔憂推
升殖利率曲線上行 。從短、中、長天期殖利
率/存續期間比率來看 ，美國短天期債具備利
率波動下最大抗震能力 ，而長天期承受 利率
上行耐受幅度相對僅 50個基本點 ，考量利率
風險可能持續至年底 ，債市投資人可配置中
短天期債券降擾動。
對等關稅宣布後，內需防禦股獲利下修幅度最少 美國中短天期投資級債年至今表現相對較穩美國大型企業淨利率創1998年來新高

美國大型企業債信仍強，中短天期投資級債為首選

### Page 8

8
股市
 全球主要國家債務攀升 ，利率創高添股市評價修正風險 ，加上科技股舉債投入資本支出 ，未來獲利
與變現能力備受關注 。美伊衝突加劇不確定性 ，短線可透過布局醫療與公用事業等防禦股 ，降低市
場震盪風險 ，科技及AI半導體等發展趨勢不變 ，獲利等基本面支持長線發展 ，採逢回分批布局 ，並
分散國家與產業配置。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，全球利率彈升有利銀行股 ，歐、日銀行股基本面佳 ，日本半導體股
分批布局較穩健，歐洲國防軍工仍具投資吸引力。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融、工業等產業機會
區域：日本銀行股、日本半導體股、歐洲
金融、國防
債市
 發債供給與聯準會升息預期 ，美國公債殖利率續升，應避免超長天期債券投資 ，預期年底前美國10
年期公債利率將於4.5%-5.0%震盪，可逢利率走升時 ，以中短天期債券進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體
質較佳，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引力者 ，包含金融、科技、通訊及公

用事業等產業。
 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。
債種：中天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
 聯準會9月升息預期持續，美元短線高檔震盪。
 日本銀行釋出鷹派訊號，9月升息可能性升高，有利日圓止貶回升。
日圓震盪偏升
商品
 中東衝突反覆 ，荷姆茲海峽短期恢復正常通行機率偏低 ，加上石油庫存探低 ，預期今年能源仍呈供
不應求態勢，油價維持偏高水位。
 美國財務壓力與去美元化之兩個長期結構性問題依然存在，央行買盤提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。
短期黃金區間操作，中長期維持偏多看法
類別 市場看法 偏好資產
策略思維
策略思維 債務供給與升息預期擾動，基本面形塑中長期布局契機
